# 8x Assignment — Agent Capture Setup

Paste this entire file into your coding agent as your **first message**, before any
other work on the assignment. Do not start building until the check in step 4 passes.

We are not checking whether you used AI. We assume you did. We are checking *how* you
work with it, and the only way to see that is the raw prompt-and-response record.

---

## 1. Identify your setup

State, in your first reply:

- The tool you are using (Claude Code, Cursor, Codex CLI, Windsurf, Aider, other)
- The exact model or models, including which one plans and which one executes
- Whether that tool has a hook, lifecycle-event, or rules mechanism that can run a
  command automatically on every prompt and every response

If you do not know whether your tool has one, look it up before answering. Do not
guess, and do not fall back to manual logging until you have checked.

---

## 2. Install the capture hook

Set up automatic capture for your tool. **Whatever the mechanism, it must fire on its
own.** If you have to remember to run it, it is wrong.

Roughly, by tool:

- **Claude Code** — hooks in `.claude/settings.json` in the repo. Wire the prompt event
  and the end-of-turn event to a script that appends to the log. The end-of-turn hook
  receives a path to the session transcript on stdin.
- **Cursor / Windsurf** — a project rules file, plus whatever export or history
  mechanism the app provides. Check whether the app writes a session store on disk you
  can read from.
- **Codex CLI / Aider / other** — check for a session log, a transcript flag, or a
  config hook. Most write a session file somewhere; find it and extract from it.

If your tool genuinely has no automatic mechanism, say so explicitly, name what you
checked, and wrap the session instead. Saying "my tool cannot do this" without having
checked is the wrong answer.

---

## 3. What to capture

Write captures to `.agent-logs/` in the repo root. These are committed and ship with
your repo, publicly, so treat them as part of the submission.

**We want the prompt and the final response. Nothing in between.**

Not the thinking. Not the tool calls. Not the intermediate steps, the file reads, the
diffs, or the retries the model made on its own. Just: what you asked, and what came
back at the end of that turn.

Capture, per turn:

- The prompt, verbatim and in full. No truncation, no paraphrase, no cleanup.
- The final response for that prompt, in full, including the ones where it got it wrong.
- A UTC timestamp.
- The model name, so a switch mid-build is visible.

Do not:

- Add `.agent-logs/` to `.gitignore`. It ships with the repo.
- Edit, tidy, summarise, or delete an entry after the fact. A messy honest log scores
  better than a clean one, and we can tell the difference.

Commit the logs as you go, interleaved with the code they produced, not in one dump at
the end. The commit order shows the order you actually worked in.

### Format

Match this. It is the format we use internally, so it is the one we can read fastest.
One file per session, named `YYYY-MM-DD_HH-MM-SS_<session-id>.md`.

Do not start the assignment until you have done this.

2. Confirm the prompt **and** the response both appear in `.agent-logs/`

4. Create `CAPTURE-TEST.md` in the repo root containing:

   - Anything you tried first that did not work

`CAPTURE-TEST.md` is the first thing we open. If capture is not working the rest of the
submission is not assessable, so get this green before you build anything.

---

## 5. Then build

Once capture is verified, start the assignment in the same repo. Everything from here
is recorded automatically and you can stop thinking about it.

impressive. Dead ends, wrong turns, and the moment you realised the first approach was
wrong are the most useful things in it. Leave them in.

## 1. Setup

- **Tool:** Claude Code, desktop app (`CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT=claude-desktop`, bundled binary 2.1.255; the `claude` CLI on your PATH is 2.1.241).
- **Model:** `claude-opus-5` (Opus 5), for both planning and execution. There is no separate planning model — your `~/.claude/settings.json` sets no `model` override (just `effortLevel: high`), and I'm not delegating to subagents. The only other model that ran was `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` in a throwaway headless probe session I used to inspect raw hook payloads — before the hook existed, with an isolated `--settings` file, so it's not in the logs.
- **Hook mechanism:** yes. I checked the current hooks reference (code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks) and then verified empirically on this install: Claude Code has `UserPromptSubmit` (receives `prompt` verbatim + `prompt_id` + `transcript_path`) and `Stop` (receives `last_assistant_message`, the same `prompt_id`, and `transcript_path`). Neither carries a model name — I confirmed that from the actual stdin payloads — so the model is read from `message.model` on the assistant lines of the session transcript JSONL.

## 2. What's installed (commit `bcb248d`)

- [.claude/settings.json](.claude/settings.json) — `UserPromptSubmit` → `capture.sh prompt`, `Stop` → `capture.sh stop` (+ `SessionStart`/`PostModelSwitch` → `capture.sh hint`, best-effort model hint for a session's first prompt).
- [.claude/hooks/capture.py](.claude/hooks/capture.py) — appends PROMPT / RESPONSE entries to `.agent-logs/YYYY-MM-DD_HH-MM-SS_<session-id>.md` in your exact format; skips thinking, tool calls, tool results, and intermediate text. Append-only; only the frontmatter counters are rewritten. Never prints to stdout, always exits 0, errors go to a gitignored `capture-errors.log`.
- [.claude/hooks/capture.sh](.claude/hooks/capture.sh) — launcher that finds a `python3` without trusting the app's PATH.
- [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md) — tells every future session to commit `.agent-logs/` with the code and never touch the entries.
- Repo initialised on `main` (the folder wasn't a git repo). `.agent-logs/` is **not** in `.gitignore`.

Offline test in a scratch dir passed: paired turn, multi-line prompt containing `---` and a fake `[LOG_ENTRY …]` line, orphan-recovery path, and both Python 3.13 and system 3.9.

one more thing before any product code. codebase rules. put them in CLAUDE.md under "Engineering rules" (dont touch the capture section) and add eslint rules for whatever can be linted: max-lines, max-depth, max-params, no-restricted-imports, import/no-cycle. if a rule blocks shipping inside the window, break it but say so in the commit message.

structure
- feature first folders: src/features/<domain>/{components,server,schemas,constants.ts}. domains: auth, marketplace, campaigns, collaborations, tracking, payouts
- layers: app (routes) -> features/*/components (views) -> features/*/server (queries + actions) -> db. dont skip layers. views never import from @/db, enforce with no-restricted-imports
- page.tsx is thin. call a query, render a feature component. under 100 lines, no business logic
- src/lib is pure functions only (fit score, money, dates). no io, no db imports
- src/components/ui = shadcn only, dont edit. shared composites in src/components
- max 500 lines per file, split components around 300. max ~10 files per folder, after that make a subfolder
- one component per file, file name = component name. hooks are useX.ts. no utils.ts / helpers.ts, name files by what they do

boundaries
- server components by default. 'use client' only if the file needs state, effects or handlers
- anything touching the service role key starts with import 'server-only'
- data crosses boundaries as plain DTOs not drizzle rows. one toDto per entity
- mutations only via server actions in features/*/server/actions.ts, zod validated, return { ok, data } or { ok: false, error }. never throw to the client
- reads only via features/*/server/queries.ts. no queries inside components
- collaboration status only changes through transition(). a direct status write is a bug
- zod schemas in features/*/schemas.ts are the single source of truth for form, action and dto type

clean code
- strict ts, no any. non-null assertions need a comment
- functions do one thing. over 40 lines split it. over 3 params use an options object. max nesting 3, early returns
- no magic numbers, constants.ts per feature
- comments say why not what. no commented out code
- import/no-cycle on

views
- every list / dashboard screen has a loading skeleton, an empty state with a cta, and an error state. no exceptions
- tailwind theme tokens only, no raw hex
- forms = react-hook-form + the shared zod schema. optimistic ui on accept / approve / pay with rollback
- real buttons and links, labelled inputs, focus states, keyboard reachable. no div buttons
- check 375px and 1440px before committing a screen
- no mock data in components, seeds only

data
- every table: id, created_at, updated_at. money in integer cents. timestamptz utc
- migrations only via drizzle-kit, never hand edit one
- index every fk and every hot path where column (tracking_links.code)
- redirect handler = one insert + one 302, nothing else

process
- conventional commits, small, message says why. .agent-logs in every commit
- typecheck + lint + test green before push
- deploy after every feature and check it on the live url not localhost. /api/health first
- new env var = .env.example updated in the same commit
- half done stuff behind NEXT_PUBLIC_FF_* flags, not commented out routes
- errors logged server side with context, user gets a friendly message, no stack traces in the browser
- keep README current: how to run, architecture, whats real vs stubbed

tests
- unit tests for pure logic: fitScore, transition, money, attribution matching
- one integration test for redirect -> click -> lead
- no snapshot tests, dont test framework glue

done means: ci green, deployed, checked on the live url, committed with the log, README + .env.example updated. if any of that is missing say which and why.

commit as "chore: engineering rules + lint enforcement" then wait for my next prompt

**What's in it**

- [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md) — new "Engineering rules" section with your rules as written, plus a closing "Not lintable (reviewed by hand)" list so it's explicit which ones lint can't catch. The capture section is byte-identical (verified with `diff`).
- [eslint.config.mjs](eslint.config.mjs) — built on `eslint-config-next`'s flat presets. Enforced:
  - `max-lines` 500 everywhere, 100 on `app/**/page.tsx` + `layout.tsx`
  - `max-depth` 3, `max-params` 3, `max-lines-per-function` 40 (**warn**, since JSX components blow past 40 routinely — the 500/100 line caps are the hard stops)
  - `no-explicit-any` error, `no-non-null-assertion` warn (can't lint "has a comment", so it surfaces each one)
  - views-never-touch-db and lib-is-pure via `no-restricted-imports` (alias imports) **and** `import/no-restricted-paths` (catches relative `../../db` too)
  - `import/no-cycle`, `no-magic-numbers` in `features/*/server` + `src/lib`
  - `jsx-a11y` for real buttons/links/labels/focus; raw hex colours rejected in `.tsx`
  - `src/components/ui` exempt from all size/shape rules

**Validated, not assumed.** Scratch install of eslint 9.39.5 / eslint-config-next 16.3.5 / next 16.3.5 with fixture files breaking each rule: every rule fired, the shadcn fixture (600 lines, 5 params, hex) stayed clean. That caught two real bugs before commit — a duplicate pattern ESLint's schema rejects, and a zone glob needing `/**` to match files. Both are in the commit message.

**Caveats, also in the commit message**

- The config can't run *in this repo* until the scaffold adds `package.json`. The versions above are what the scaffold should pin.
- `git add -A` swept in `docs/reference/naano-market-notes.md`, which you'd added by hand — it's in this commit rather than its own.

**Commits on `main`** (pushed): `1f0525d` scaffold → `952a124` drizzle + supabase + health → `b4607f1` ci + vitest → `056271d` readme + your reference screenshots. The `.agent-logs` change went into the first of these — the log only changes once per turn, so within a single turn only one commit can carry it.

**Env vars to paste into Vercel** (Project → Settings → Environment Variables, all environments) and into `.env.local` — same list with the same comments is in [.env.example](.env.example):

| Variable | Where it lives in Supabase |
| --- | --- |
| `DATABASE_URL` | Top bar **Connect** → ORMs / Connection string → **Transaction pooler** (port 6543). Swap in the DB password from Project Settings → Database. |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` | Project Settings → **Data API** → Project URL |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` | Project Settings → **API Keys** → `anon` (legacy tab) or the `sb_publishable_…` key — either works |
| `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` | Project Settings → **API Keys** → `service_role` (legacy tab) or the `sb_secret_…` key. Server-only. |

`VERCEL_GIT_COMMIT_SHA` is injected by Vercel automatically. After you add the vars, redeploy and hit `/api/health` — it should return 200.

**Two things that didn't go to plan**

1. **Push-to-deploy isn't wired.** `vercel git connect` was refused: the Vercel GitHub app has no access to `m-faizananwar/naano-rebuild` — a permission grant only you can make. Fix: Vercel dashboard → project `naano-rebuild` (team `zune-dev`) → Settings → Git → connect the repo (it'll prompt to install the GitHub app). Until then, I deploy from the CLI (`vercel deploy --prod`) after each feature, which is what I did here. If you'd rather not grant the app, a GitHub Action with a `VERCEL_TOKEN` secret is the alternative.
2. **`vercel link` created a `.env.local`** containing only `VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN`. It's gitignored; paste your values into it as planned.

**Deviations from the rules, on record:** `/api/health/route.ts` imports `@/db` directly via an explicit lint override — its whole job is to prove the db is reachable, and there's no domain it belongs to. Committed as `27a7735` (your docs changes + `docs/plan.md`); push is running in the background (56 MB of screenshots). The plan, verbatim from that file:

## 1. Data model

All tables `id, created_at, updated_at`; money in EUR cents.

- **users** (email, password_hash, role, names) · **sessions** (token_hash, expires_at)
- **brands** (owner, company, website, value_prop, icps[3], target_industries[], target_regions[], wallet_cents, pixel_site_key)
- **creators** (user, linkedin_url, headline, bio, country, industries ≤3, followers, price_cents, bundles[], median_views, engagement_rate, posts_per_month, audience_job_titles{}, audience_seniority{})
- **campaigns** (brand, name, status, open_to_applications, post_deadline, default_fee_cents, brief jsonb in the editor's exact shape: what_to_tell, target_industries, target_geos, tone, do[], avoid[], links[], angles[{angle, hook, direction, example}])
- **collaborations** (campaign, creator, origin `invitation|application`, status, fee_cents, due_date, revision_round, draft_text, post_url, scheduled_at, published_at, paid_at) — unique (campaign, creator)
- **collaboration_events** (from, to, actor, note) — written by every `transition()`; the audit trail
- **tracking_links** (collaboration, code unique+indexed, destination) · **clicks** (link, ts, ip_hash, ua, referrer)
- **pixel_events** (site_key, `visit|signup|purchase`, value_cents, click nullable) · **ledger** (party, `topup|booking|payout|withdrawal`, amount, collaboration) — wallet, earnings, invoices are views over it · **shortlist** · **messages**

`fitScore` is computed, never stored: audience overlap, category match, engagement quality, posting consistency — each a signal with a one-line reason.

**State machine** (only transitions the map shows):

| from | to | who |
|---|---|---|
| — | `invited` | brand |
| — | `applied` | creator |
| `invited` | `accepted` / `declined` | creator |
| `applied` | `accepted` / `declined` | brand |
| `accepted` | `draft_submitted` | creator ("Draft ready") |
| `draft_submitted` | `approved` / `changes_requested` | brand (the review modal: "approving or requesting changes", bounded by revision rounds) |
| `changes_requested` | `draft_submitted` | creator |
| `approved` | `scheduled` | creator (sets date) |
| `scheduled` | `live` | creator (post URL — self-reported, no LinkedIn API) |
| `live` | `paid` | system when the wallet covers it; brand "Pay" if short |

Terminal: `declined`, `paid`. Tabs map directly (brand "Invitations received" = `applied`, creator "Needs action" = `invited ∪ changes_requested ∪ approved`). Not modelled because not in the map: cancel after acceptance, disputes, refunds.

## 2. Build order (grader's click order)

1. **Docker Postgres + schema + seed** — demo brand mid-campaign with a collaboration in *every* state, ~40 creators with audience snapshots, click history. Real rows from hour 2.
2. **Own auth + both app shells, every sidebar tab routed** — thin but real loading/empty/error states; nothing 404s.
3. **Login with "Enter as demo brand / demo creator"** — graders won't sign up.
4. **Landing page, one pass** — it's the first paint.
5. **Brand › Creators** — cards (fit %, CPM, median views, filters) + profile modal + shortlist — the product's thumbnail.
6. **Brand › Campaigns** — AI brief with template fallback → editor → invite ⇒ `invited`.
7. **Creator › Opportunities + Apply; Collaborations (six tabs); collaboration detail** = the state-machine actions.
8. **Brand › Collaborations + review-draft modal + pay ⇒ ledger; creator schedule → live.** Loop closes; everything after is leverage.
9. **Tracking link, `/r/[code]` (one insert, one 302), Results, creator Analytics** — numbers move when the grader clicks.
10. **Billing + Earnings** from the ledger — money on both sides.
11. **Onboarding** — creator live card + `recommendPrice` + bundles; brand site → value prop → ICPs → starter brief. Showy, cheap, after the loop.
12. **Attribution layer** — click log + CSV per creator · `/n.js` with naano's API on a demo landing page · fit % as signals + reason · pre-spend estimator (tier CTR × vertical CPL, confidence label).
13. **Thin tabs filled** — Messages (real thread per accepted collab), Community (leaderboard from seed), Affiliate, Settings, Integrations (static), notifications.
14. **Nao matching chat** over fit-scored top-N with fallback — last.

**Out:** Stripe, email + 6-digit codes, LinkedIn/Apify import, X/YouTube, FR, agency mode, MCP endpoint, calendar booking, invoice PDFs, team invites, cookie banner, the floating AI pill.

## 3. The trap

The screenshots are of an *empty account*. Cloning them faithfully yields a product made of empty states, and the reflex is to fill dashboards with hardcoded numbers — which reproduces naano's own public weakness (attribution nobody can audit) instead of beating it. Two halves: the loop is two-sided, so every brand action needs a creator counterpart screen or you've built "100% of a landing page"; and any number on a dashboard must come from a row the grader can create by clicking a tracking link. Seed + state machine + one click table are the product; the AI brief and marketplace gloss are the paint.

---

**Assumptions I'm proceeding on (say otherwise and I'll change them):**
- **Staying on Next 16**, not 15 — it's already scaffolded, building and deployed; nothing in the plan needs 15 and a downgrade costs time for no feature.
- Supabase clients and their env vars get **removed** in the auth commit; only `DATABASE_URL` and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (optional) remain.
- Local DB: `docker compose` Postgres 16 at `postgresql://postgres:postgres@localhost:5432/naano`; `.env.local` gets that value.
- Demo logins seeded as `brand@demo.naano` / `creator@demo.naano`, password `demo1234`, cookie sessions with a hashed token, passwords via `bcryptjs`.
- `live` is self-reported (post URL); `paid` fires automatically when the wallet covers the fee.
- The plan doc lives at `docs/plan.md` so future sessions and the grader can read it; I'll update it if the order changes.

Starting item 1 (Docker + schema + seed) unless you redirect.

## What's live

**Core loop, end to end (every number is a row):** brand finds creators → books or negotiates (funded invitation, fee held from the wallet) → creator accepts → submits draft → brand reviews (approve / request changes, 2 rounds) → creator schedules → publishes with the LinkedIn URL → tracked link `/r/{code}` (one insert, one 302) → `/n.js` pixel on `/demo/landing` attributes visits/sign-ups/purchases to the click → Results / campaign analytics / click-log CSV → brand pays → creator's earnings release → withdrawal. Verified in the browser and in SQL on `main`.

- **A — Brand › Creators**: marketplace (300 creators, fit % vs the selected campaign, URL-state filters/sort/search, 24/page "Show more", persistent shortlist), profile modal (audience bars, reach chart, latest post, fit breakdown + reason, book rail), "Your selection" + "Make an offer" dialogs, AI Matching (Nao) with rationale + trade-off line.
- **B — Brand › Campaigns**: cards with real counts, chooser (AI chat with history rail / link / team stub), 4-step launch stepper with the benchmark estimator, brief editor with naano's exact fields, detail tabs (Collaborations · Brief · Shortlist · Analytics with daily clicks), launch-plan popover (now mounted in the top bar).
- **C — Collaboration loop**: creator opportunities + apply, brief drawer (Copy as Markdown / Copy for my AI), six-tab tables both sides, detail pages whose actions come from `allowedEvents(status, role)`, "Review LinkedIn post" modal, pay, messages (thread per booking, optimistic send, NaanoBot placeholder).
- **D — Public site + auth visuals**: landing page in the map's section order, for-creators/agencies/pricing/faq/case-study/about/benchmarks, register/login restyled to the screenshots, branded 404.
- **Integrator (me)**: tracking (redirect, pixel, collector, demo landing, Results + CSV), payouts (earnings + withdrawal, billing ledger), creator analytics, both overviews, settings with real updates, integrations (3 MCP dialogs verbatim), community leaderboard, affiliate, tour, invite, creator card + public `/c/{handle}`, row-derived notifications, the shell fix for 375px tables.

## What's stubbed

Add-budget dialog (deferred per your addendum; Billing shows the ledger, top-up buttons disabled with a note) · Stripe / bank rails (withdrawal writes a ledger row) · email (no 6-digit code, no team invites — the forms say so) · LinkedIn/Google sign-in (visual) · link-based campaign creation doesn't fetch the URL · Claude paths for briefs and Nao are written per the `claude-api` skill but **untested against the API** (no key on any machine; template fallback is what runs) · book-a-call (no calendar) · NaanoBot (canned) · MCP endpoint (setup dialogs only) · Slack link · affiliate counters stay 0 (ref not stored) · onboarding steps (website analysis, creator card 2–4) · FR, agency mode, X/YouTube.

## What each stream assumed

- **A**: `/brand/creators` opens on Marketplace, AI Matching at `/brand/creators/matching`; 24/page (spec) not 50 (inventory); "engagers" count derived from median views; bundle booking = one collaboration with the bundle total; seed "UK" → GB flag; euros shown naano-style ("188 €").
- **B**: stepper at `/brand/campaigns/{id}/launch`, picks carried in the URL; fee capped at €1,500; `claude-sonnet-5` via structured outputs, re-validated with the shared zod schema; committed budget = pending + completed bookings; est. reach = median views of published posts; native `<select>`s.
- **C**: CSRF token echoed on every mutation (beyond Next's Origin check); strangers get 404 not 403; action panel driven by server `allowedEvents`, optimistic badge only; seeded invitations have no `acceptBy` (new ones do); no system auto-pay — the brand's Pay button is the path.
- **D**: 8 FAQ answers, plan detail lines, about/benchmarks caveats written from the map; landing case-study numbers (9/2,940/512) differ from naano's own case-study page — both shown; Managed plan shows €700/month + "custom quote"; footer only links to routes that exist; `--brand-soft/--brand-sky/--linkedin/--success` tokens added.
- **Me**: a `workspace` domain beyond the six for the account surface; `createCollaboration` now refuses inactive/closed campaigns (C's observation); `src/lib/dates.ts` was an add/add conflict between B and C — merged with a union `toDateInputValue`; `/c/{handle}` returns 404 without a database rather than the "not configured" state.

- The stream worktrees are at `/Users/nexxus/Projects/naano-streams/stream-{a,b,c,d}` (branches `stream/*`, all merged); each has its own `node_modules` — delete when you're done inspecting.

after E and F merge and before the polish pass: motion. right now the clone is static and naano isn't, and that's the first thing you feel side by side. these are pulled from their css, copy the numbers, don't invent easings.

their keyframes and transitions, verbatim:
- sectionAppear: 0% opacity 0 translateY(20px) → 100% opacity 1 translateY(0). used as .animate-section, 0.35s ease-in forwards.
- .reveal: transition opacity 0.8s cubic-bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1), transform 0.8s same curve. sections reveal on scroll into view.
- .glass-card: transition 0.4s cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1). cards lift on hover, shadow deepens.
- a[href], button: transition opacity 0.15s, transform 0.15s. hover opacity, active press scale ~0.98.
- drawChart: stroke-dashoffset → 0, 2.5s ease-out, 0.5s delay, forwards. every line chart draws itself on mount (results, campaign analytics, creator analytics, profile modal reach chart).
- shimmer: translateX(-100%) → translateX(100%). skeletons and the "naano is improving your shortlist" progress strip.
- localeIn: translateY(100%) opacity 0 → translateY(0) opacity 1, 0.3s cubic-bezier(0.2, 0, 0, 1). the EN/FR switch slides the label up.
- pulse (50% opacity 0.5), spin, bounce, and radix accordion-down / accordion-up (height 0 ↔ content height) for the faq.

implement: css keyframes in globals plus tailwind utilities, a tiny useReveal hook with intersectionobserver for the landing sections, framer-motion only for the drawer / modal / popover enter-exit if it's already cheaper than css for you, and prefers-reduced-motion respected everywhere. apply them here:

landing: every section reveals on scroll (reveal). hero copy and buttons sectionAppear on load. logo strip slow horizontal marquee. the five step how-it-works mini cards stagger in 60ms apart. post example cards and pricing cards lift on hover (glass-card). faq accordion with accordion-down/up.

auth + onboarding: step content sectionAppear on each step change. the marketplace card preview updates with a 200ms crossfade on every field change. "reading your profile…" button and card chip pulse. the Data ——— Pending bar animates its fill. card back flip on "more details" is a 3d rotateY 0.5s with backface hidden. brand onboarding "analyze my website" shows a determinate progress bar over ~20s with three status lines that swap in with localeIn (reading your site… finding your icps… drafting the brief…), then "opening ai matching…" state on the continue button.

app shells: route content sectionAppear on navigation. sidebar collapse 0.2s width transition, labels fade. hover on nav items background 0.15s. notifications and account menus fade + translateY(-4px) 0.15s. coach mark popover fade in 0.25s with a subtle scale from 0.96. get started ring animates its stroke to the current step.

marketplace: cards stagger in 40ms apart on load and on filter change, lift on hover. fit % counts up from 0 on first paint (300ms). profile modal fade + scale 0.98→1 in 0.2s, reach chart draws (drawChart). audience bars fill from 0 on open. bookmark star pops (scale 1.2 then 1, 150ms). "naano is improving your shortlist" strip: shimmer plus a progress bar that runs for ~4s then collapses.

ai matching: the cloud illustration floats (slow 6s translateY ±6px loop). "got it — i'm searching…" line appears, then a typing dots indicator for ~2s, then the rationale paragraph fades in, then the four rows stagger in 80ms apart. thumbs / copy icons hover opacity.

collaborations: status chips crossfade on transition. optimistic actions: button goes to a spinner, row updates, toast slides in from bottom right 0.25s and out after 3s. brief drawer slides in from the right 0.3s cubic-bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1) with a backdrop fade. review modal fade + scale.

results and billing: number tiles count up on mount. chart draws. csv export button shows a 600ms spinner then the download. add budget dialog: wallet chip value counts up to the new balance.

the floating assistant pill, both shells: bottom centre, pill with the spinner glyph on the left, placeholder text that cycles every 4s between "what would you like to do?", "what can i help you find?", "what would you like to see?" using localeIn, a waveform mic button on the right, a small chevron above it that collapses the pill to a dot. hover lifts it 2px. clicking opens the nao box on brand and the help sheet on creator with the same fade + scale.

one rule: nothing over 0.8s except the chart draw and the onboarding progress, nothing that blocks a click, and every animation has a reduced-motion fallback that just shows the end state. commit as feat(motion): … in two or three commits by area. then the polish pass and the report as planned.

- **Core loop, both sides** — marketplace with fit %, funded booking/offer dialogs, campaigns (chooser, AI chat, link, 4-step launch with the estimator, brief editor, detail tabs, delete), opportunities/apply, collaborations with every state-machine action, review modal, pay, messages.
- **Attribution** — `/r/{code}` → `/demo/landing` with the real `/n.js` pixel → Results/analytics/CSV. Every dashboard number is a row.
- **Onboarding** — brand (real site read: registering with vercel.com produced "Vercel", a value prop, 3 ICPs, a "Vercel creator brief" campaign, GET STARTED 1/3, coach mark); creator (simulated LinkedIn read, live card, €490/€315 recommendations, bundles, professional info, tour).

- **Shell** — collapsible sidebar, MCP pill, wallet chip link, launch-plan ring, notifications from `collaboration_events`, account menus, agency toggle (visual), floating pill with cycling placeholder.
- **Motion** — three `feat(motion)` commits (`4ab6fc7`, `2a75bba`, `708ae5f`): naano's keyframes/easings verbatim, reveal-on-scroll with a geometry fallback, staggers, count-ups, chart draws, retuned dialog/sheet/menu timings, reduced-motion end states everywhere.

## What's stubbed / assumed per stream

- **D (public):** FAQ answers and some page copy written by the agent; case-study numbers disagree at the source (shown both); footer drops `llms.txt`.
- **E (creator onboarding):** LinkedIn read is deterministic from the slug; card flag appears after step 3; professional info stored, never enforced.
- **F (brand onboarding):** company from the site title; failed reads keep the registered name; `claude-sonnet-5`; JS-only sites fall to the template.
- **G (dialogs/shell):** its final report never arrived (the agent stalled twice and was resumed); its three commits merged cleanly and I verified the top-up, withdraw dialog, pill, bell, menus, book-a-call slot picker, Deal Link dialog and six-client integrations grid by hand. Its inventory audit is folded into the list below.
- **Me:** `workspace` domain added beyond the six; `/api/health` is the one route allowed to import `@/db`; Next 16 kept over the requested 15; Zune's seed copy is naano's real onboarding output.

Live at `b0b86ac`, and health now confirms it: `ai: "template"`, `voice: "web-speech"`.

## Report

### Phase one — fidelity fixes (commits `7d2db62` → `ebb324d`)
All seven items built and verified locally with the CDP driver; the code-side ones are live. Three items are **seed-dependent and still show old data on the live URL** (CPM 11–34 €, the two open untouched campaigns, recommended rows on the creator overview) because `.env.remote.local` isn't in this checkout — run `pnpm db:seed:remote` once and they appear.

### Phase two — voice (commits `4bfff26`, `016b1e8`, `b79c5e8`, `b0b86ac`)
- **`src/features/voice`**: one zod intent schema (11 tools + unknown), `POST /api/voice/intent` (Claude structured output when `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` exists, otherwise the regex grammar in [voice-grammar.ts](src/lib/voice-grammar.ts) — response carries `source`), executor that maps spoken names → ids and runs the **existing** actions (`bookCreator`, `reviewDraft`, `topUpWallet`, `applyToCampaign`, `submitDraft`) with the viewer's session + CSRF.
- **Confirmation gate**: money/status tools answer `"… Confirm?"` + a `pending` intent; only a spoken yes runs it, no cancels, anything else is a new command. Role guards refuse brand tools as a creator and vice versa before the gate.
- **Providers**: Web Speech API by default; Vapi when both keys are set — `/api/voice/session` creates/updates the assistant from code (`ensureAssistant`, one `command({transcript, confirmed})` tool, server URL `/api/voice/vapi` + derived secret) and mints a one-hour headless session; the webhook forwards into `/api/voice/intent` with that session so both providers share one path.
- **Pill**: mic button on the right of the floating pill with listening (waveform + streaming transcript), thinking, speaking, confirming ("Say yes or no") and error states; reducer in [voice-state.ts](src/lib/voice-state.ts). Checked at 1440 and 375.
- [docs/voice.md](docs/voice.md), `.env.example` (`NEXT_PUBLIC_VAPI_PUBLIC_KEY`, `VAPI_PRIVATE_KEY`), README voice row. 89 tests green.

### Verified on the live URL
- `/api/voice/intent`: 401 anonymous, 403 wrong CSRF; navigate → `/brand/campaigns`; search → `/brand/creators?country=Germany&max=800`; results; unknown → spoken "Sorry"; top-up ask → **no** → "Cancelled." (wallet unchanged) → ask → **yes** → wallet moved; creator navigate/card/submit-draft ask; role guards both ways.
- Pill state machine with a fake `SpeechRecognition` feeding synthetic transcripts: listening → thinking → speaking → idle with route change; gated no/yes flows including `confirming`; error recovery.

### Not exercised
- The Claude parse path and the Vapi path: no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or Vapi keys on the live project or here (health shows `ai: template`, `voice: web-speech`). Both are written against the same SDK patterns already used in the repo (`messages.parse` + `zodOutputFormat`; Vapi's documented tool-call payload).
- "apply to product teams" fails live only because the remote DB predates the new open campaigns (same reseed note as above).

One assumption to flag: a Vapi reply can't navigate the page (the webhook has no browser), so on that path navigation is spoken only — documented in `docs/voice.md` and the README.

## Report — `fix(public): splash hard cap 2.5s`, live at `91eecaf`

- **Budget from first paint, not hydration.** The script measures `first-contentful-paint` and forces 100 at 1730 ms at the latest (2500 − 450 out − 200 fade − 120 ms overhead margin), skipping the 80 ms hold; the number only reads the preload progress and nothing waits on the blob's `attach` (settle = fonts ready + preload complete, or the cap).
- **Compositor-side guard, independent of JS.** A CSS animation takes the ring + number out at 1.73 s and fades the overlay to `visibility: hidden` at 2.2 s → gone by 2.4 s even when hydration arrives late; the script then just unmounts it without ever locking scroll. Pages without a splash enter immediately (the earlier nav gate would have held the header on `/for-creators` — fixed in the same commit).
- **Root cause of the starved timers.** The 17 blurred full-section canvas draws for the card chrome ran on mount and jammed the main thread; they now start after the splash, when idle, and only once a card is near the viewport.
- **Verified** ([LandingSplash.tsx](src/features/public/components/splash/LandingSplash.tsx), [splash.module.css](src/features/public/components/splash/splash.module.css)): locally, five cold loads with cache disabled → hidden 1.96–2.50 s after FCP; **live**, three cold loads → hidden **2389–2397 ms** after FCP, 100 % forced at ~1.75 s when JS is in time.

One thing outside this fix, flagged to Faizan: on cold loads the live FCP itself lands 1–6 s after navigation because of the render-blocking Google Fonts / onlinewebfonts stylesheet links — separate from the splash, and cheap to make non-blocking if wanted.

hero scrub bug, high priority, before anything else in your queue: scrolling through the hero feels slow and laggy. it's a landing page, not the standalone, so tune the port (leave public/hero/index.html exactly as spec):

1. the track is too long. 560vh means five and a half screens of scrolling for a 10s clip. set the landing wrapper to 260vh, min 1600px. retime the cues to the shorter track so panels 2 and 3 still get their dead zones: [0,0,.18,.26], [.36,.44,.58,.66], [.76,.84,1.1,1.2].
2. the eased seek is too sluggish for a scroll. on the landing use seekAt += gap * 0.24 (the spec's 0.115 stays in the standalone) and snap when |gap| &lt; 0.004s.
3. the real culprit is probably the source. until the 11mb blob finishes, the port has nothing attached (spec waits for blob or 15s bail), and after the splash's 2.5s cap the user is scrolling an empty or network-seeking hero. fix: ffmpeg the clip once into public/hero/hero-scrub.mp4 as all-intra (-an -c:v libx264 -preset fast -crf 20 -g 1 -pix_fmt yuv420p -movflags +faststart) at 1600px wide, and attach that local file immediately on mount with preload="auto" so seeking works from the first scroll; keep the blob preload going in the background and, when it resolves, swap to the blob URL at the same currentTime in one frame with the paused frame held (our port, not the standalone, so the "never swap src" rule doesn't bind). serve it from public/ with long cache headers (vercel.json header for /hero/*).
4. use requestVideoFrameCallback when present to only issue the next currentTime after the previous frame painted, so fast wheels don't queue seeks; fall back to the rAF loop.
5. panels: opacity/transform only, will-change set, no layout in the loop — check with a performance recording that the scroll stays at 60fps at 1440 on the deployed build.

verify on the live url: scroll the hero top to bottom in ~1.5s and confirm the video tracks the scroll without stutter and panel 3 is fully visible before the next section. commit as fix(public): hero scrub responsiveness, deploy, report.

2. Easing 0.24 with a 4 ms snap (standalone keeps 0.115).
3. [public/hero/hero-scrub.mp4](public/hero/hero-scrub.mp4) — all-intra, 1600 px, faststart (derived by [scripts/backdrops.mjs](scripts/backdrops.mjs)) — attached on mount with `preload="auto"`; [vercel.json](vercel.json) gives `/hero/*` and `/backdrops/*` immutable cache headers. The CDN blob loads behind it and swaps in at the same `currentTime` between scrolls with the paused frame held on a canvas; a failed/stalled blob reverts to the local clip.
4. `requestVideoFrameCallback` paces seeks (released by `seeked` or 120 ms too — a seek onto the frame already shown never presents a new one).
5. The real hog: the hero glass card re-rasterised the SVG filter over a viewport-sized canvas every rAF. It now covers the card + 96 px, redraws only on new video frames, capped at 20 fps, zero cost at rest.

**`fix(public): footer video + bottom-row clearance`** — `FooterMedia` loads/plays the clip when the footer is near (autoplay was left paused at 0 on the long page), poster painted as the wrapper background, `referrerpolicy="no-referrer"`; paint order and reduced-motion were not at fault; standalone plays on its own. 96 px extra row padding on the landing; the cookies pill was removed upstream meanwhile, so only the padding applies — all three legal links hit-test visible at 1440 and 375.

**Two extra first-load fixes** found while verifying: the say-hey section's 13 MB `preload="auto"` clip (`ede20c1`) and the metric stage's ~31 MB of clips (`2406b52`) started at HTML parse and starved the page's chunks and the hero on the origin connection; both are lazy now, and the hero downloads in sequence (local clip first, blob after it's 98 % buffered). One slip on the way: a rebase conflict resolution took the wrong side and pushed a non-building commit (`0131c78`), fixed within minutes by `1c5c63b`.

**Verified** on the local production build with GPU headless (Apple M4): 60 fps through a 1.5 s top-to-bottom scrub, the video trailing by a constant 0.26 s and landing on the last frame; panel 3 fully visible from progress .84 with its headline well above the fold.

Shipped as `0680be3`, live (health reports it; note the deployment now answers at **amplio-mvp.vercel.app** — the old naano-rebuild-opal host 307s there).

**The premise didn't hold, so the fix is a different one than ordered.** [session.ts:49](src/features/auth/server/session.ts:49) is `createHeadlessSession` — the 1-hour row minted for the Vapi voice token, which never gets a cookie. Demo logins go through `createSession` exactly like register/login/reset ([actions.ts:76/90/109](src/features/auth/server/actions.ts:76)), so demo rows already get `SESSION_TTL_DAYS` = 30 days, matching the cookie. I left `HOUR_MS` in place (the voice token should stay short-lived) and added a comment saying so.

What actually bounces a tab an hour in: the session **row** disappears while the cookie remains — most likely the remote reseed truncating `sessions` (a real expiry does the same). The proxy only checks the cookie exists, the shell then finds no viewer, redirects to `/login`, and the stale cookie lingers. Now:

- [shell-viewer.ts:43](src/features/auth/server/shell-viewer.ts:43) — no viewer → redirect to `/api/auth/expired?next=…` instead of `/login` directly.
- New [route.ts](src/app/api/auth/expired/route.ts) deletes `amplio_session` and 307s to `/login?next=…` (server components can't clear cookies during render, hence the hop).

Verified live with a bogus cookie: `/brand` → 307 `/api/auth/expired?next=%2Fbrand` → 307 `/login?next=%2Fbrand` with `set-cookie: amplio_session=; Expires=1970`. Typecheck, lint, 95 tests and a prod build green; `.agent-logs` in the commit.

If Faizan does see a demo session die at exactly one hour with no reseed in between, that's a different bug and I'd want the timestamp — nothing in the code produces it.

Keys I was unsure about:

1. **`results.pixel.dialog.body`**: `&lt;/head&gt;` is wrapped as `'&lt;/head&gt;'` in both languages. Without that, next-intl reads it as an unmatched closing tag and fails. It displays as `&lt;/head&gt;` through `t()`, but `t.raw()` would show the quote marks. `{global}('track', …)` displays correctly.
2. **`results.chart.description`**: `{total}` arrives as a formatted string, so there is no plural. A total of 1 would read "1 qualified clicks" / "1 clics qualifiés". To fix that, pass a number or add a plural on a count.
3. **`results.log.description`**: EN puts the plural on `{count}` ("Latest {shown} of # clicks"). FR puts it on `{shown}`, because in French the noun follows that number ("Les # clics les plus récents sur {count}"). Both need `shown` and `count` as numbers.
4. **`results.numbers.reach.hint`**: "Estimate: sum of median views · # live post(s)". This says it's an estimate and how it's calculated, but it's long for a hint. The short option is "# live posts" alone, since the label already says "Estimated".
5. **`results.posts.columns.reactions` / `comments`**: I used "Avg. reactions" / "Avg. comments" (FR "Réactions moy." / "Commentaires moy.") so the table itself doesn't suggest these are the sponsored post's numbers. Plain "Reactions" / "Comments" is fine if the description is always visible.
6. **`results.chart.ranges.*`**: "Week / Month / Year" ("Semaine / Mois / Année"). If the ranges are rolling 7, 30 and 365 days, "7 days / 30 days / 12 months" would be more accurate.
7. **`results.numbers.spend.label`**: I kept the overview's "Committed to creators" for consistency. Change it if this page is meant to show money actually paid out.
8. **`results.pixel.copied` / `pixel.view` / `pixel.dialog.copy`**: "Snippet" is translated as "code" in French ("Code copié", "Voir le code"). "Extrait de code" is more literal but long.</result>

May I make a small edit to your PageHeader for the brief's "word-by-word heading reveal"? Or would you rather do it?

Why I'm asking: my RouteEnter can't do it safely. Splitting an h1's text nodes into spans after hydration mutates DOM that React owns, and the next render would fight it.

What I'd add: an internal `<WordReveal text={title} />` that renders each word as `<span style="--w:i" class="word-in">` with a normal space between them. The words are real React output, so there's no DOM mutation. CSS in interaction.css: opacity 0 → 1 plus translateY(6px) → 0, 180ms on ease-ledger, 30ms per word, capped at about 8 words. The result is a static heading under reduced motion and an unchanged accessible name. That makes every page title enter word by word on both sides, and RouteEnter stops marking h1 so the two don't double up.

Say yes and I'll do it (a one-line change in PageHeader plus the component), or no and I'll leave headings as they are.

Also on main: 791cebc removes unused motion components (AnimatedStat, Beam, HoverBeam, CyclingText, TypingDots, Reveal, useMotionDialog). Nothing of yours imported them.

- **Billing**: shows available, held, committed and paid money; top-up is still a no-card demo.
- **EN / FR**: now real French (cookie-based, no URL change). The agency toggle, integrations page, MCP mention and "real FR locale" under "Not built" are removed.

The public pages are static now. They used to render fresh on every request and are now served from Vercel's cache in English and French. The rest of the promppter's queue is also on main and checked on amplio-mvp.vercel.app. That checking was read-only; anything that writes data was tested on localhost only.

**The biggest change: public pages served from cache (f25f7c3).** Before, every public page rendered fresh on every request: no caching, 0.6–0.8 s to first byte. Now:
- The public and sign-in pages are built ahead of time in English and French, and URLs don't change. The language comes from the language cookie, then the browser's setting.
- The landing, pricing and for-creators pages refresh their numbers at most once a minute.
- Live, every one of those pages is a cache hit from the nearest edge after the first request.
- Time to first byte from this machine is about 0.34 s, but that's the connection's floor: a plain icon file takes 0.32 s, and the server itself answers in about 0.13 s.
- First paint in a headless browser was 0.3–0.9 s at both widths, but a few runs took 3.5–4.5 s. The server was a steady cache hit throughout, so I believe those spikes are from the test browser or this network, not the site. I didn't confirm that.
- It exposed a real bug: every list in the French text (legal sections, FAQ) was being turned into something that broke the page, so the French terms page errored. Fixed.

**Other changes:**
- **Theme (c997934):** follows the operating system by default. There's a light/dark/system switch in the top bar, the public nav and the account menu, and no flash on first load.
- **Stat cards (66cb7cd):** the caption sits on its own line, "Show rows" sits bottom-right, and cards in a row are the same height.
- **Decisions doc (7ae1472):** DECISIONS.md lists the extra cuts and a new "Stopped pretending" section, collected from all three builders.
- **Copy test (88e480e):** a new test fails if a brand or creator page shows text that isn't in the translation files. It found five such strings, now moved.
- **From the other builders' QA:**
  - A missing message on Results caused a console error (5d6581b).
  - Releasing a payment now takes two clicks.
  - Estimated reach shows "—" instead of a 0 when a creator's views aren't known (0393711).
- **Cleanups and extras:**
  - Old design leftovers and an unused dependency are removed.
  - /dev/ui shows the animation and graphics kit.
  - The Overview's to-do list shows each item's state icon.
  - The README table of what's real and what's stubbed is current.
  - The Overview screenshots were retaken on live.

**Not done:**
- The "—" reach state isn't verified on live. The demo data has no creator with unknown views, and I didn't change data to create one.
- The small celebration animation when a to-do item is cleared wasn't added. Items are cleared on the collaboration page, not on the Overview, so the Overview never sees the moment.

The Sanity connector still needs authorizing in your connector settings, and the gpt-bridge connector still wasn't reachable. Neither was needed for this work.

**The opening** now says what Amplio does:

**The check they asked for:** that a fresh production build never serves cached data left by the previous deploy. I tested it with two production builds on my machine against the local database, sharing one cache folder:
1. Build A saved three cached entries.
2. Build B saved new entries instead of reusing A's, so the keys differ between deploys.

| Served as | Clicks returned | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| Build B's deploy ID | 640 | Read from the database; A's entry ignored |
| A's deploy ID (control) | 987654 | A's fake entry served |

So each deploy only reads its own cached data. On Vercel the key uses the deploy ID Vercel sets on every deployment, falling back to the commit ID.

<cross-session-message from="local_6a013c76-8710-4ee8-b24f-040a8ef6bf05" name="builder clone (fork)">
Bug in a36489f: a Link styled with buttonVariants() (a blob tone) renders at rest as a solid ink pill with an invisible label.

Where I see it at 375:
- /reset-password/<bad token>: "Request a new link" is a black pill with no text.
- The same applies to CtaLinks on /pricing, /for-creators and /faq, and to the onboarding wizard's Back link.

Cause, as far as I can tell: links have no .blob-btn__inner, the white inner surface that sits above the offset-shadow ::after on a <button>. On a link, the ink ::after paints over the link's own white background, and the label is ink on ink. getComputedStyle still reports a white background, which is why it's easy to miss.

Possible fix in src/styles/blob-button.css, for `.blob-btn:not(:has(.blob-btn__inner))`: give it `isolation: isolate` and put its `::after` at `z-index: -1`, so the shadow stays under the pill. Or drop the ::after for links.

I haven't touched your file. Tell me if you'd rather I patch it. On my side, the wizard's back/forward are otherwise fine as blob buttons.
</cross-session-message>

<cross-session-message from="local_f46baa94-92fd-43fd-b346-92ae63fcbcc6" name="naano promppter">
New round for you (design system). The owner wants richer dropdowns, one opening animation shared by every dropdown in the app, a new account pill, new fonts and more tactile micro-interactions. Write every line yourself: match the behaviour and values below, never copy code from anywhere. Small conventional commits to main, .agent-logs riding along, files under 500 lines, i18n EN + FR for any copy, reduced motion shows final states instantly, check at 1440 and 375 in light and dark. Feature cutoff 17:30 UTC, hard freeze 19:00 UTC. Rules unchanged: push only main, no `vercel deploy`, live checks read-only. The fork owns auth + onboarding and is working there now (hint texts, picture step, customers dialog, password eye, wizard font), so don't edit those files; everything else in the app is yours.

1. ONE DROPDOWN MOTION, EVERYWHERE. Build a single popover primitive and move every select, combobox, multi-select, filter pill, account menu, notifications menu, language/theme menu, row "…" menu and date picker onto it, so they all open the same way:
- The panel grows out of the corner nearest its trigger. It renders at full size and is revealed by a clip-path, from `inset(0 92% 92% 0 round 16px)` (for a panel below and left-aligned; mirror the insets for top or end alignment) to `inset(0 0 0 0 round 16px)`, over 320 ms with ease-out cubic-bezier(.16,1,.3,1). At the same time, on an outer wrapper, opacity 0→1 and scale .96→1 on a spring (~500 ms perceived, a slight bounce of about .22), with transform-origin at that same corner. Closing plays it in reverse, a bit faster.
- The shadow is a `drop-shadow(0 10px 18px rgba(0,0,0,.14))` filter on the wrapper, so it hugs the clipped shape (a box-shadow would be clipped away). Radius 16, white surface (dark: surface token), hairline border.
- Rows arrive in sequence: each option fades in from translateY(-6px) over 340 ms, 35 ms after the one before, capped at the 8th row (the rest arrive with the 8th).
- The trigger's chevron sits in a round grey disc (28px inside a 44px field, 36px inside a 52px pill), and the disc turns a half turn on a spring while open. Its hover darkens the disc slightly. Use a drawn chevron path, not a double-headed arrow.
- The selected option shows a check that draws in (stroke-dashoffset), and options have a hover well. For single selects, the panel opens so the current choice sits exactly over the trigger when there's room (fall back to below). It flips above when there's no room below, stays 8px inside the viewport, has full keyboard support (arrows, Home/End, typeahead, Esc returns focus) and correct listbox/menu roles.
- Reduced motion: no travel or clip; a 120 ms fade only.

2. THE ACCOUNT PILL (top bar, right side). A white pill 52px tall, radius 26, padding 0 6px, gap 11px, lifted off the page by a soft neutral shadow (no border ring), with a deeper shadow on hover. Inside: a 40px round avatar in a warm accent tint (the user's uploaded picture, else a drawn silhouette), then two lines, the person's name (13px, weight 450, soft ink) above the company or creator handle (15px, weight 700, ink, truncated with an ellipsis), then the chevron in a 36px grey disc. Max width 268px; on phones it collapses to the 52px avatar only. On click, the pill itself morphs into the menu: the same white surface grows downward and to 268px wide, pinned to its right edge so it grows inward, with radius 26→22 and a spring layout animation. Under the header come the email (12px, soft, one line) and menu rows (36px tall, radius 10, 13.5px weight 450, the icon at 1.5 stroke, a hover plate): Profile, Settings, Language, Theme, Sign out. The bar doesn't reflow while it opens (reserve the closed pill's box). Esc and an outside click close it, and it morphs back.

3. FONTS (next/font, self-hosted, variable where possible). App UI and auth pages: Instrument Sans (400–700). The sidebar only: Inter (variable; 450 for a resting row, 600 for the current one). Numbers, amounts, IDs and dates: JetBrains Mono, tabular. Leave the landing and public pages' fonts alone, and leave the onboarding wizard to the fork (it takes Plus Jakarta Sans). Update DIRECTION.md's Type section to say which face goes where and why (in our own words: the sidebar face fits 14px labels in a narrow column, and so on).

4. MICRO-INTERACTIONS, our own, where they already fit (don't add new features):
- Segmented controls and tabs: the pill stretches toward the new pick like an inchworm (the leading edge moves first, then the trailing edge catches up), and labels recolour as the edge crosses them.
- Save buttons: shrink into a spinner while saving, then show a check, or shake on error.
- Copy buttons (links, referral link, pixel snippet): the two pages merge into one and a check draws in.
- Counters and badges (notifications, wallet, Needs you): digits roll in the direction of the change and the badge bumps. The notification bell swings when something new arrives.
- Toasts: stacked with depth, fanning out on hover, each one's edge draining as its timer runs.
- Tooltips on icon buttons: one tooltip slides from button to button and reshapes around each label.
- Collapsible sidebar: labels step aside before it folds to the icon rail.
- Number steppers (budget, fee, followers): digits roll and it speeds up while held.
- Skeletons that hold the exact shape of what's coming, so nothing jumps.
- Settings: an autosave dot that splits into three while saving, then gathers into a check.

Report with the commit list and screenshots at 1440 and 375, light and dark: a select open, a combobox open, the account pill closed and open, a row menu open, the notifications menu open, and a page showing the new fonts.
</cross-session-message>

Found the dev "1 Issue" the owner sees. It's React's "Encountered a script tag while rendering React component. Scripts inside React components are never executed when rendering on the client." It fires whenever a client navigation mounts a component that renders an inline <script>. Three places:
1. src/i18n/HtmlLang.tsx: fired on /register → /onboarding/brand/website after sign-up. I fixed it (committing now; it's a one-file change in your i18n folder, flagging so it doesn't surprise you). It's now a client component: it renders the inline script only while rendering on the server and hydrating that HTML (useSyncExternalStore with a server snapshot of true and a client snapshot of false), and sets documentElement.lang in an effect for client mounts. Verified: no issue after sign-up, lang still correct.
2. Yours, src/features/public/components/nav/PublicNav.tsx:14 (`<script … PRE_SCRIPT>`). Repro on `pnpm dev`: open /register, click the Terms link (or /register → Privacy). The (public) layout mounts on the client and the overlay shows 1 Issue.
3. Yours, src/features/public/components/hero/GlassHero.tsx:60 (`<script … TIMELINE_SCRIPT>`). Repro: open /pricing and click the logo / Home link to "/". The landing mounts client-side and shows 1 Issue.
Besides the warning, on those client navs the scripts never run, so check the hero isn't held at its first frame (PRE_SCRIPT) after a client nav. The same pattern as HtmlLang would fix both: script only for server HTML and hydration, and an effect (or no-op) on client mount. Other than these, a click-through of every creator and brand page (EN/FR) on dev is clean.
</cross-session-message>

A real one for you, shown as a dev console error ("A tree hydrated but some attributes of the server rendered HTML didn't match the client properties"). On `pnpm dev` of current main, load /creator (and probably /brand). React's diff points into src/components/ui/ring-widget.tsx: RingWidget > svg[viewBox 0 0 200 200] > LinkComponent aria-label "Needs you: 2" > <a> > <line> ×N. So the tick lines' x1/y1/x2/y2 (or a style) render differently on server and client. It's most likely float formatting from Math.cos/sin (full-precision numbers stringify differently across the two runtimes), or a value derived from state/measurement (the `ref={{current:null}}` div) during render. Rounding the coordinates (e.g. `.toFixed(2)` or Math.round(x*100)/100) and not reading layout or `window` during render should clear it. It's the only hydration error in a sweep of my creator pages.
</cross-session-message>

Add this to your round, before the micro-interactions: THE DESCRIPTION FIELD. Every multi-line text in the app (company description, what customers care about, creator bio/headline, campaign brief, offer notes, review feedback, draft text, settings) uses one shared Textarea that looks like this. Write it yourself, with no new dependency.
- Layout: label above (13px, 600), an optional one-line context under the label (12px, soft ink), then the box, then the message line below (error, or a warning), all padded 4px in from the box edge.
- The box: white, hairline zinc-200 border, radius 22, padding 14px 16px, 15px text at 1.55 line height, min 3 rows. It grows with its text (`field-sizing: content`, with a JS auto-height fallback where unsupported, capped at ~14 rows before it scrolls) and has no resize grip. Hover: the border darkens one step. Focus: the same 4px soft accent ring as the other fields, a 1px lift on the spring, and border/shadow transitions of 250 ms.
- While focused, a light beam travels around its border: a short bright arc of the accent green (fading to transparent at both ends) circling the rounded border once every ~3 s, drawn as a masked conic-gradient ring on a pseudo-element, with its angle animated through a registered `＠property`. It fades in over 250 ms on focus and out on blur. Under reduced motion there's no beam; the static accent ring stays.
- A character count bottom-right inside the box (12px mono, soft), shown when a max length is set, turning amber within 10% of the limit and red over it.
- If the text still contains a `[bracketed]` example (from AI-drafted text or a template), a warning line under it says "Replace the [bracketed] parts before saving" (EN + FR). It's a warning, not an error, and it disappears as soon as the brackets go. The server refuses to save such text with the same rule, where it matters (brand description, brief).
- Invalid: a red ring and a message with a small icon, as for inputs.
Put it in /dev/ui with an empty, a filled, a focused, a near-limit, a bracketed-warning and an error state. Then replace every existing textarea outside auth/onboarding with it. New feature round: AGENT MODE, the interface (you). The 3rd is building the backend: a Gemini function-calling agent that talks with a brand or a creator (text or voice call), asks what it needs, shows each step and does the job with every tool, with money and collaboration changes behind a confirm card. The 3rd will push an event contract at src/features/agent/events.ts within ~20 minutes (step, question with chips, result cards, confirm, streamed message, done, error) plus fixtures. Build the UI against the fixtures until the stream is live. Code cutoff 18:15 UTC, freeze 19:00; it all sits behind the AGENT_MODE flag so nothing existing breaks. Same rules: small commits to main, gate green, files under 500 lines, EN + FR, reduced motion, 1440/375 light and dark, your own code.

1. THE AGENT PAGE: /brand/agent and /creator/agent, in the app shell, with a sidebar item "Agent" (a sparkle or the three-dot mark), and ⌘K "Ask the agent". Layout: a centred conversation column (max ~760px) with the composer pinned at the bottom (the description Textarea look, one row growing; send; mic; a "Call" button that switches to the voice view). On wide screens a right rail shows "What I know" (the brand profile or creator card summary, the saved notes it remembers with a remove on each) and the thread list to resume.

2. RENDER EVERY EVENT AS UI, not text walls:
- `step`: a timeline row with the TrailDots loader while running, then a check, or a red mark on failure. It expands to show the input and output summary (a receipt: "Searched creators · B2B · France · ≤ €500 → 12 found"). Consecutive steps group under a collapsible "Worked for 4 steps".
- `question`: the assistant's question with quick-reply chips (clicking one sends it).
- `result`: cards per kind. creators = a horizontal scroll of creator cards (avatar, name, headline, followers, price, fit chip that opens its reasons, Shortlist and Open buttons). brand = a brand-details card (logo, value proposition, the three customers). campaign = a brief card. collaborations = compact rows with next action. wallet/earnings = stat cards with rolling numbers. opportunities = cards with Apply.
- `confirm`: the ActionDialog look inline, as a card with a toned header, the facts in mono (the total held from the wallet, per creator), and Confirm (blob, money tone) plus Change. Confirming calls /api/agent/confirm and the card turns into a stamped receipt ("Booked · €1,240 held").
- `message`: streamed text, word by word.
- Errors are an honest line with a retry.
- Empty first screen: a greeting and 3–4 starter prompts per role (brand: "Find creators for my next campaign", "What needs me today?", "How is my Q4 campaign doing?"; creator: "Find campaigns that fit me", "What do I need to do this week?", "How much have I earned?").

3. VOICE VIEW: the existing call UI, driven by the same stream. The mark's three dots are the visualizer (listening, thinking, speaking); a live caption of what it's saying; step rows appear under it; a confirm card appears big with Confirm and Cancel, and saying "yes" confirms.

4. FIRST-RUN EMPTY STATES (lists with nothing yet: campaigns, collaborations, opportunities, messages): a masked row of example cards slowly sliding behind a raised skeleton card, then a title, one line and the primary button. Make one FirstRun component and use it where the current empty states are thin.

Report with 1440/375 screenshots of a brand run (steps, the creators result, the confirm, the receipt), a creator run and the voice view.

<cross-session-message from="uds:/tmp/cc-socks/19369.sock" from-session="local_1c186ddd-5b2a-4a7d-8377-c585924c32cc" from-name="builder clone (fork) 3rd" from-mode="bypass">
The contract is on main, 4ce78de. It differs from the brief's sketch in a few places:
- Types: `@/features/agent/events` (agentEvent / AgentEvent, stepEvent, resultEvent, confirmEvent, creatorCard). Fixtures: `@/features/agent/fixtures` (BRAND_RUN_FIXTURE, CREATOR_RUN_FIXTURE, validated in events.test.ts).
- Shapes vs your list:
  - result is a discriminated union on `kind` with `title` plus `items` (creators, collaborations, opportunities) or `item` (creator, brand, campaign, wallet, earnings), not a generic `data`.
  - confirm carries `facts: [{label, value, cents?}]`, `confirmLabel`, `cancelLabel` and `expiresAt`. There's no totalCents: the money lines have `cents`.
  - message is `{id, text, final}`. Chunks of one message share an id; `final: true` closes it.
  - done is `{threadId}` (null when threads can't be stored). error is `{message, retryable}`.
- Transport: SSE, not NDJSON. POST /api/agent with `{text, threadId?, history?, locale?}` returns `text/event-stream`, one `data: <json>\n\n` frame per event (`sse()` in events.ts). Read it with fetch plus a reader and split on `\n\n`. Confirm: POST /api/agent/confirm with `{id, threadId?, decision: "confirm"|"cancel"}`; that also streams SSE (the continuation after the action). The field is `id`, not confirmId.
- Flag: agreed, `NEXT_PUBLIC_FF_AGENT_MODE`. My routes return 404 unless it's "1", so one name gates both sides. Local on, prod on only once it passes.
- `history` is sent only when the server can't store threads; done's threadId === null tells you to keep and resend the last turns yourself.
I won't touch agent/components/** or the pages.
</cross-session-message>

<cross-session-message from="uds:/tmp/cc-socks/19369.sock" from-session="local_1c186ddd-5b2a-4a7d-8377-c585924c32cc" from-name="builder clone (fork) 3rd" from-mode="bypass">
The agent backend is on main, ec9d458: POST /api/agent and POST /api/agent/confirm. Three things your client must do:
1. Send `x-csrf-token: <viewer.csrfToken>` on both POSTs (same as /api/assistant/chat). Without it you get 403 "This session is stale".
2. Read `text/event-stream`: split on "\n\n", strip "data: ", then JSON.parse. Every turn ends with `done` (threadId null for now, so keep the last turns and send them as `history`) or `error` {message, retryable}. Non-2xx statuses are plain JSON errors: 401 not signed in, 403 csrf, 404 flag off, 429 rate limit, 400 bad body.
3. Confirm with body `{id, decision: "confirm"|"cancel"}`. The id is single-use and bound to the user and session; a second use answers "That has already been done." The confirm stream is: a step (running → done/failed, label "Doing …") and then the agent's closing message.
Behaviour to expect: a turn can end on a `question` (with chips) and no message; after a `confirm` card, the message is "It's ready. Confirm and I'll do it." Result cards arrive right after their step. Real run on the local DB: brand "find me 3 French B2B SaaS creators under €500 for my Q4 campaign and book them" → listCampaigns → searchCreators → creators card (6) → bookCreators confirm (3 creators, €615 held, wallet after) → confirm → "3 of 3 invitations sent". Tell me if anything in your renderer disagrees with what actually arrives.
</cross-session-message>
